# Recovery Workbench — AI engineering standards (v2)

This is the standards file the coding agent reads before every task, as defined in
`Recovery Workbench AI Assisted Development Approach v1` (section 3). It applies to the recovery
module of the ACE service and to anyone using an AI tool on this repository.

**Owner:** Solution architect with the TechX tech leads.
**Change control:** pull request, reviewed like code.
**Baseline:** document set v14, 16 November 2026. The implementation evidence from 10 November 2026
(`RWB-E1-042`, `RWB-TE-001`) is preserved; both are re-specified against this baseline.
**Source:** `requirement/legacy/08-ai-and-agents/recovery-workbench-ai-assisted-development-approach-v1.md`
and `requirement/legacy/11-delivery-and-operations/recovery-workbench-engineering-practices-v5-docx.md`.

## 1. The seven working rules

1. The person who merges a change owns it, whoever or whatever wrote it.
2. The agent works from files: the standards file, the story file and the plan file. Anything the
   agent needs is in one of the three.
3. The tests and the acceptance criteria exist before the code is generated.
4. Every line that reaches production is read by a person.
5. An AI error found by a reviewer is written into this file, so the agent is told not to repeat it.
6. No personal data or production data goes into a prompt, a test or a log.
7. The unit of work is one story. Work is merged at least once a working day.

## 2. Structure and stack

- The Workbench is the recovery module of the ACE service: one service, one repository, one front end.
- Back end: Spring Boot (Java 21), Spring JPA, Spring Batch, Spring Security with method security,
  Spring Session, Spring Cloud Azure, Spring Data REST, Spring HATEOAS and Quartz.
- Front end: React on Vite with Radix and TanStack Query. Front-end types are generated from the
  back end.
- Database: PostgreSQL, schema `recovery` in the ACE database, referenced only as
  `${recoverySchema}` and fixed to `recovery` in every environment (D-RCV-28). Schema changes use
  versioned migrations, one migration per story, forward-only. Files are named
  `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`, where `<seq>` is a four-digit sequence taken in merge order and line 1
  is `-- Story: <story key>`.
- Domain driven design, RESTful API, end-to-end type safety and validation.
- Cases are modelled in CMMN 1.1 terms: case, stage, milestone, task, sentry, event listener and
  case file item. From Epic 3 the case lifecycle runs on Temporal; case data stays in the recovery
  schema (D-RCV-36, ADR-011).
- Rules are versioned data, authored as Drools XLS decision-table packages and evaluated by embedded
  Drools (D-RCV-36, ADR-012).
- Hosting: the ACE namespace on the shared ACE AKS clusters; Temporal is self-hosted in the same
  namespace.

## 3. The agent must

1. Put recovery code in the recovery module folder only. Change the common or collection module only
   where the story file names it.
2. Read every threshold, limit, timer, queue, role, template and route from its versioned reference
   table. Never write one as a literal in code.
3. Put a Spring Security method check on every endpoint, naming the capability area and the operation.
4. Write a decision record entry for every change to a case and every decision, with the rule ID and
   version used.
5. Use the names in the business glossary for classes, fields, tables and endpoints.
6. Return each validation message from the message table by its rule ID.
7. Make database changes by migration script only, one script per story. Name it
   `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`; line 1 is `-- Story: <story key>`. A corrective script takes the
   next sequence, keeps the original story key and adds line 2 `-- Fix: <reason>` (D-RCV-28).
8. Use Spring Batch and Quartz for every Epic 1...

Instructions from: C:\Users\SahilMore\Downloads\Recovery Workbench SharePoint Set v14\rwb-wt\RWB-E1-044\AGENTS.md
# Recovery Workbench — AI engineering standards (v2)

This is the standards file the coding agent reads before every task, as defined in
`Recovery Workbench AI Assisted Development Approach v1` (section 3). It applies to the recovery
module of the ACE service and to anyone using an AI tool on this repository.

**Owner:** Solution architect with the TechX tech leads.
**Change control:** pull request, reviewed like code.
**Baseline:** document set v14, 16 November 2026. The implementation evidence from 10 November 2026
(`RWB-E1-042`, `RWB-TE-001`) is preserved; both are re-specified against this baseline.
**Source:** `requirement/legacy/08-ai-and-agents/recovery-workbench-ai-assisted-development-approach-v1.md`
and `requirement/legacy/11-delivery-and-operations/recovery-workbench-engineering-practices-v5-docx.md`.

## 1. The seven working rules

1. The person who merges a change owns it, whoever or whatever wrote it.
2. The agent works from files: the standards file, the story file and the plan file. Anything the
   agent needs is in one of the three.
3. The tests and the acceptance criteria exist before the code is generated.
4. Every line that reaches production is read by a person.
5. An AI error found by a reviewer is written into this file, so the agent is told not to repeat it.
6. No personal data or production data goes into a prompt, a test or a log.
7. The unit of work is one story. Work is merged at least once a working day.

## 2. Structure and stack

- The Workbench is the recovery module of the ACE service: one service, one repository, one front end.
- Back end: Spring Boot (Java 21), Spring JPA, Spring Batch, Spring Security with method security,
  Spring Session, Spring Cloud Azure, Spring Data REST, Spring HATEOAS and Quartz.
- Front end: React on Vite with Radix and TanStack Query. Front-end types are generated from the
  back end.
- Database: PostgreSQL, schema `recovery` in the ACE database, referenced only as
  `${recoverySchema}` and fixed to `recovery` in every environment (D-RCV-28). Schema changes use
  versioned migrations, one migration per story, forward-only. Files are named
  `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`, where `<seq>` is a four-digit sequence taken in merge order and line 1
  is `-- Story: <story key>`.
- Domain driven design, RESTful API, end-to-end type safety and validation.
- Cases are modelled in CMMN 1.1 terms: case, stage, milestone, task, sentry, event listener and
  case file item. From Epic 3 the case lifecycle runs on Temporal; case data stays in the recovery
  schema (D-RCV-36, ADR-011).
- Rules are versioned data, authored as Drools XLS decision-table packages and evaluated by embedded
  Drools (D-RCV-36, ADR-012).
- Hosting: the ACE namespace on the shared ACE AKS clusters; Temporal is self-hosted in the same
  namespace.

## 3. The agent must

1. Put recovery code in the recovery module folder only. Change the common or collection module only
   where the story file names it.
2. Read every threshold, limit, timer, queue, role, template and route from its versioned reference
   table. Never write one as a literal in code.
3. Put a Spring Security method check on every endpoint, naming the capability area and the operation.
4. Write a decision record entry for every change to a case and every decision, with the rule ID and
   version used.
5. Use the names in the business glossary for classes, fields, tables and endpoints.
6. Return each validation message from the message table by its rule ID.
7. Make database changes by migration script only, one script per story. Name it
   `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`; line 1 is `-- Story: <story key>`. A corrective script takes the
   next sequence, keeps the original story key and adds line 2 `-- Fix: <reason>` (D-RCV-28).
8. Use Spring Batch and Quartz for every Epic 1 and Epi...

```text
-- Local only. Run by 10-load-ace-database.sh, not by the Postgres entrypoint (hence .sql.inc).
-- Applies the ADR-010 read boundary in the catalog, so a direct read of a public table by the
-- recovery service fails at the database and not only in code review.

-- The recovery schema and its tables belong to the migration account.
ALTER SCHEMA recovery OWNER TO rwb_migrator;
GRANT USAGE, CREATE ON SCHEMA recovery TO ace_view_owner;   -- CREATE is needed to own the views

-- ace_view_owner reads ACE; nobody else does.

GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public TO ace_view_owner;

END $$;
```

```text
1: #!/bin/sh
2: # Local only. Creates the roles and databases of the ACE database estate on one Postgres server.
3: #
4: #   ace                  ACE database: schema public (ACE team) and schema recovery (Workbench)
5: #   temporal             Temporal persistence, owned by temporal_svc, no grant on ace (ADR-011)
6: #   temporal_visibility  Temporal visibility, owned by temporal_svc
7: #
8: # Roles (ADR-010, ADR-011):
9: #   rwb_migrator    runs recovery Flyway, owns the recovery tables
10: #   recovery_svc    the recovery service identity: DML on recovery tables, SELECT on v_ace_* views,
11: #                   no privilege in public
12: #   ace_view_owner  owns the v_ace_* views, holds SELECT on the ACE public tables, cannot log in
13: #   temporal_svc    owns the two Temporal databases, cannot connect to ace
14: set -eu
15: 
16: psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 --username "$POSTGRES_USER" --dbname postgres <<SQL
17: CREATE ROLE rwb_migrator   LOGIN PASSWORD '${RWB_MIGRATOR_PASSWORD}';
18: CREATE ROLE recovery_svc   LOGIN PASSWORD '${RECOVERY_SVC_PASSWORD}';
19: CREATE ROLE ace_view_owner NOLOGIN;
20: CREATE ROLE temporal_svc   LOGIN PASSWORD '${TEMPORAL_SVC_PASSWORD}';
21: 
22: CREATE DATABASE ace;
23: CREATE DATABASE temporal            OWNER temporal_svc;
24: CREATE DATABASE temporal_visibility OWNER temporal_svc;
25: 
26: REVOKE CONNECT ON DATABASE ace, temporal, temporal_visibility FROM PUBLIC;
27: GRANT  CONNECT ON DATABASE ace TO rwb_migrator, recovery_svc;
28: GRANT  CONNECT ON DATABASE temporal, temporal_visibility TO temporal_svc;
29: 
30: ALTER ROLE recovery_svc SET search_path = recovery;
31: ALTER ROLE rwb_migrator SET search_path = recovery;
32: SQL
```

```text
// Mirrors the ACE monorepo backend layout so recovery module code moves across unchanged:
// package th.co.scb.ace.demo.recovery, migrations in db/modulemigration/recovery/.
```

```text
# Recovery migrations

Flyway location for the recovery module. History table `flyway_schema_history_recovery`.

Rules (D-RCV-28, BS-2.2 to BS-2.6, checked by `RecoveryMigrationLintTest`):

- File name `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`, 4-digit contiguous sequence taken at merge, for example
  `V0001__rwb_e1_001_recovery_schema.sql`.
- Line 1 `-- Story: <story key>`. A corrective file takes the next sequence, keeps the story key and
  adds line 2 `-- Fix: <reason>`.
- Always `${recoverySchema}`, never a literal `recovery.`, `ace.` or `public.`; no `SET search_path`.
- No `R__` files, no `GRANT`/`REVOKE`, never edit a merged file.

The folder is empty on purpose. Locally the schema comes from Physical Schema v6, loaded by
`infra/postgres/init/10-load-ace-database.sh`. When RWB-E1-001 ships V0001 to V0003, set
`RWB_LOAD_REFERENCE_SCHEMA=false` and Flyway builds the schema from here.
```

#### NameRule
```java
public final class NameRule implements MigrationRule {
	public String ruleId() { return "L-NAME"; }
	public List<MigrationFinding> check(ParsedMigrationFile file, FolderContext folder) {
		if (ParsedMigrationFile.isUndoName(file.fileName()) || ParsedMigrationFile.isRepeatableName(file.fileName())) return List.of();
		if (file.hasVersionedName()) return List.of();
		return List.of(new MigrationFinding(ruleId(), file.fileName(), 0, file.fileName(),
			"name must be V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql"));
	}
	public List<MigrationFinding> checkFolder(FolderContext folder) {
		List<MigrationFinding> findings = new ArrayList<>();
		for (String name : folder.otherEntries()) {
			if ("README.md".equals(name)) continue;
			findings.add(new MigrationFinding(ruleId(), name, 0, name, "only .sql migration files and README.md belong here"));
		}
		return findings;
	}
}
```
Note: `isUndoName` returns true for `U...sql`; `isRepeatableName` for `R__...`.

```text
<#
.SYNOPSIS
  Local Recovery Workbench stack: one entry point for the day-to-day commands.

.EXAMPLE
  ./scripts/dev.ps1 up            # Postgres, Temporal, Temporal UI, Azurite, mock Entra ID
  ./scripts/dev.ps1 up -App       # ... plus the backend and frontend containers
  ./scripts/dev.ps1 backend       # bootRun against the stack (profile local)
  ./scripts/dev.ps1 frontend      # Vite dev server on :5173

  ./scripts/dev.ps1 e2e           # API e2e (Gradle) + browser e2e (Playwright) against the stack
  ./scripts/dev.ps1 status        # container state and the platform health checks
  ./scripts/dev.ps1 down          # stop, keep data
  ./scripts/dev.ps1 reset         # stop and delete the database and Blob volumes
#>
param(
  [Parameter(Position = 0)]
  [ValidateSet("up", "down", "reset", "status", "backend", "frontend", "test", "it", "e2e", "logs")]
  [string]$Command = "up",
  [switch]$App
)

$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"
$Root = Split-Path -Parent $PSScriptRoot
$EnvFile = Join-Path $Root ".env"

function Initialize-EnvFile {
  if (-not (Test-Path $EnvFile)) {
    Copy-Item (Join-Path $Root ".env.example") $EnvFile
    Write-Host "Created .env from .env.example"
  }
  $content = Get-Content $EnvFile -Raw
  if ($content -notmatch "(?m)^RWB_TEMPORAL_CODEC_KEY=.+") {
    $bytes = New-Object byte[] 32
    [System.Security.Cryptography.RandomNumberGenerator]::Create().GetBytes($bytes)
    $key = [Convert]::ToBase64String($bytes)
    $content = $content -replace "(?m)^RWB_TEMPORAL_CODEC_KEY=.*\r?\n?", ""
    Set-Content -Path $EnvFile -Value ($content.TrimEnd() + "`nRWB_TEMPORAL_CODEC_KEY=$key`n") -Encoding utf8 -NoNewline
    Write-Host "Generated a local RWB_TEMPORAL_CODEC_KEY in .env"
  }
}

function Import-EnvFile {
  foreach ($line in Get-Content $EnvFile) {
    if ($line -match "^\s*#" -or $line -notmatch "=") { continue }
    $name, $value = $line -split "=", 2
    [Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable($name.Trim(), $value.Trim(), "Process")
  }
}
```

```text
> **Source:** `04 User stories/Recovery Workbench User Stories Epic 1 Story Files v7.zip!RWB E1 044 Database migrations versioned with parameterized schema names v1.md`

# RWB-E1-044 Database migrations versioned with parameterized schema names

SCB Collections and Recovery. Henka Group, 28 October 2026.

(C) Siam Commercial Bank and Henka Pte Ltd


| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Story key | RWB-E1-044 |
| Epic | E1, release 30 November 2026 |
| Module | P3 Case and workflow |
| Feature | FEAT-CWC-24 Run versioned migrations with parameterized schema names |
| Requirement IDs | REQ-RDF-001, REQ-RDF-014 |
| BRD section | Business Requirements Epic 1, sections 3.3 and 4; Business Requirements Epic 2, section 4; Business Requirements Program, section 5.1 |
| L3 process | All recovery processes |
| Decision | None |
| Priority | Must |
| Depends on | None |
| Status | Ready for build |

## 1. Story

As a **platform administrator (RW-PLATADM)**, I want **every schema change applied by a versioned Flyway migration, one migration per story, forward-only in PROD, with the schema name taken from a parameter**, so that **the database in every environment is the sum of known migrations and nothing else**.

## 2. Business rationale

The recovery tables share the ACE database with the collection module, and two squads change it from one repository (DT07, DT08). A column added by hand in one environment makes SIT and UAT test a database that PROD does not have. This story makes every change a numbered, checksummed migration with no schema name written in the file, and fails the pipeline on drift. It supports REQ-RDF-001 (the 14 data products held in the ACE database) and REQ-RDF-014 (stage 2 tables exist before the first treatment goes live). The measure is zero schema differences between a clean build and each environment at every release.

## 3. Preconditions

- The recovery tables are defined in the Logical Data Model and Recovery Workbench Physical Schema v6.sql.
- The recovery module folder and boundary test exist (RWB-TE-001).
- Each environment has a database account for migrations and a read-only account for the drift check, held in the key store (NFR-052).
... truncated (4,922 chars total)
```

```java
@Override
public List<MigrationFinding> check(ParsedMigrationFile file, FolderContext folder) {
    String text = file.identifierText();
    Map<String, MigrationFinding> findings = new LinkedHashMap<>();
    collect(findings, file, text, QUALIFIED, true);
    collect(findings, file, text, POSITION, false);
    collect(findings, file, text, BARE_SCHEMA, false);
    return List.copyOf(findings.values());
}

private static void collect(Map<String, MigrationFinding> findings, ParsedMigrationFile file, String text,
        Pattern pattern, boolean knownOnly) {
    Matcher matcher = pattern.matcher(text);
    while (matcher.find()) {
        String name = name(matcher);
        if (knownOnly && !KNOWN_SCHEMAS.contains(name)) {
            continue;
        }
        int line = file.lineOf(matcher.start(1));
        MigrationStatement statement = file.statements().stream()
            .filter(candidate -> candidate.startLine() == line).findFirst().orElse(null);
        if (!knownOnly && isAllowedQualifier(statement, name)) {
            continue;
        }
        findings.putIfAbsent(line + "|" + name,
            new MigrationFinding(ruleId(), file.fileName(), line, name, "literal schema " + name));
    }
}
```

1. Only literals that are part of an `EXECUTE` statement's argument are considered. Clear the `afterExecute` state at the end of that statement (the `;` at depth 0) and at the start of any new statement; do not let later `RAISE NOTICE`, `SELECT` or comment literals inherit it.
2. Concatenate adjacent literals joined by `||` into the dynamic SQL text (including `format('...')` and dollar-quoted literals).
3. Split that text on `;` (it is a string, so top-level splitting is enough), strip leading whitespace and SQL comments from each statement, and flag only if the first keyword is `GRANT` or `REVOKE`.
4. Do not flag on the word appearing later in a statement, in a quoted identifier, in a comment, or in a non-EXECUTE literal.
5. If the EXECUTE argument is a bare identifier/function/parameter (not a literal, format or concatenation), do not guess; record that limitation in the migration README and `implement/implementation-report.md` as a named residual, not as a passing test.

```java
public final class GrantRule implements MigrationRule {
    @Override public String ruleId() { return "L-GRANT"; }
    @Override public List<MigrationFinding> check(ParsedMigrationFile file, FolderContext folder) {
        TreeSet<Integer> lines = new TreeSet<>();
        List<String> codeLines = file.codeText().lines().toList();
        for (int index = 0; index < codeLines.size(); index++) {
            if (startsWithGrant(codeLines.get(index).stripLeading())) lines.add(index + 1);
        }
        for (MigrationStatement statement : file.statements()) {
            if (startsWithGrant(statement.firstWord())) lines.add(statement.startLine());
        }
        ... findings ...
    }
    private static boolean startsWithGrant(String text) { ... }
}
```

- line 2 GRANT line-start → flag.
- line 3 REVOKE line-start → flag.
- line 4 `SELECT 1; GRANT ...` → statement start → flag.
- line 5 `DO $$ BEGIN EXECUTE 'GRANT ...'; END $$;` → line starts with DO; statement first word DO → no flag. So line 5 would no longer be flagged.
- line 6 CREATE FUNCTION... → no.
- line 7 DO ... → no.

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

Fix CODE-001: `LiteralSchemaRule.statementAt` matches by start line. Need to map by offset range. `MigrationStatement` has `startOffset`, `endOffset`. The rule has `file.lineOf(matcher.start(1))` for the finding line; for the statement, we need the offset of the match. We have `matcher.start(1)` = qualifier offset. Find the statement whose `[startOffset, endOffset)` contains that offset.

Current code:
```java
int line = file.lineOf(matcher.start(1));
if (!knownOnly && isAllowedQualifier(statementAt(file, line), name)) continue;
...
private static MigrationStatement statementAt(ParsedMigrationFile file, int line) {
    return file.statements().stream().filter(statement -> statement.startLine() == line).findFirst().orElse(null);
}
```

New:
```java
int offset = matcher.start(1);
int line = file.lineOf(offset);
if (!knownOnly && isAllowedQualifier(statementAt(file, offset), name)) continue;
...
private static MigrationStatement statementAt(ParsedMigrationFile file, int offset) {
    for (MigrationStatement statement : file.statements()) {
        if (offset >= statement.startOffset() && offset < statement.endOffset()) return statement;
    }
    return null;
}
```

Careful: statements are cut at top-level `;` from the identifier text; `endOffset` is the index of the `;` (cut) exclusive. The qualifier offset falls within. For a multi-line statement, the qualifier on line 2 has an offset within the statement range → non-null.

We need a test that would have returned null before — i.e., assert the statement passed to the decision point is non-null for a qualifier on a later line. Options:
